<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every <i>η</i> &gt; 0 and every sufficiently large integer <i>N</i>, there is a polynomial with <i>N</i> consecutive coefficients in $`\{-1,1\}`$ whose modulus lies between $`\sqrt N/16`$ and $`(1+\eta)\sqrt N`$ everywhere on the unit circle.

- **claim-simultaneous-flatness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $\eta>0$ there is an integer $N_0=N_0(\eta)$ such that, for every integer $N\ge N_0$, there exist real signs $\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_{N-1}$ for which $$\frac1{16}\sqrt N \le\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\varepsilon_kz^k\right\| \le(1+\eta)\sqrt N \qquad (\|z\|=1).$$
  - Explanation: The same real sign polynomial has a uniform positive lower modulus and an upper modulus arbitrarily close to the Parseval minimum at every sufficiently large length.
- **claim-auxiliary-width-budgets** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-auxiliary)): For every $0<\delta<1/20$, there exist integers $m\ge1$ and $r\ge2$, nonzero vectors $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in\mathbb Z^m$, a real-valued trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\mathbb T^m$, and a vector $v\in\mathbb R^m$ with the following properties. No two of the vectors $a_i$ are parallel, and $$\mathcal A=\{a_1,-a_1,\ldots,a_r,-a_r\}$$ is a containing Fourier support of $F$. Moreover, $$\label{eq:auxiliary-norms} \left\lVert F\right\rVert_\infty\le1, \qquad \left\lVert F\right\rVert_2\ge1-3\delta.$$ Writing $\lambda_a=a\cdot v$ for $a\in\mathcal A$, we have $$\label{eq:auxiliary-widths} \lambda_a\ne0, \qquad \sum_{a\in\mathcal A}\|\lambda_a\|<1, \qquad \frac{\|\widehat F(a)\|}{\sqrt{\|\lambda_a\|}} \le K_\delta, \qquad K_\delta=\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}.$$ All these objects depend on $\delta$ alone.
  - Explanation: An auxiliary real trigonometric polynomial retains almost full mean-square mass while fitting its Fourier coefficients into a strict total width budget. Its containing support permits vanishing coefficients.
- **claim-uniform-quadratic-integration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic)): Let $g\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ and let $\beta\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$ be fixed. As $T\to\infty$ through positive real values, $$\label{eq:uniform-quadratic} \begin{split} \int_{\mathbb R}g(x)\mathrm e(T\beta x^2/2-ux)\,dx ={}&\frac{\mathrm e(\operatorname{sgn}(\beta)/8-u^2/(2T\beta))} {\sqrt{T\|\beta\|}}\\ &\qquad\cdot\left(g\left(\frac{u}{T\beta}\right) +O_{g,\beta}(T^{-1})\right), \end{split}$$ uniformly for every real $u$.
  - Explanation: Quadratic stationary phase gives an approximation uniform in the real translation parameter, including stationary points outside the amplitude support.
- **claim-signed-interval-packing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-packing)): Let $m\ge1$ and $r\ge2$ be integers, and let $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in\mathbb Z^m\setminus\{0\}$ be pairwise nonparallel. If $w_1,\ldots,w_r>0$ and $2\sum_{i=1}^r w_i<1$, there are an integer $H\ge1$ and points $\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_H\in\mathbb T^m$ for which the $2rH$ closed intervals $$I\bigl(\sigma a_i\cdot\theta_h,w_i/H\bigr), \qquad 1\le i\le r,\quad 1\le h\le H,\quad \sigma\in\{-1,1\},$$ are pairwise disjoint.
  - Explanation: Pairwise nonparallel integer forms admit compatible torus centers whose signed closed intervals are disjoint when their total prescribed width has strict slack.
- **claim-hypergraph-edge-partition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring)): For each integer $r\ge2$ and each $\gamma>0$, there is $\beta>0$ such that the following holds. If an $r$-uniform hypergraph satisfies, for some $D>0$, $$(1-\beta)D<d(v)<(1+\beta)D\quad\text{for every vertex }v, \qquad d(u,v)<\beta D\quad\text{for }u\ne v,$$ then its edges can be partitioned into at most $(1+\gamma)D$ matchings.
  - Explanation: Nearly regular uniform hypergraphs with sufficiently small codegrees have an edge partition into a controlled number of matchings. This is the external combinatorial input for interval packing.
- **claim-starting-wave-profile** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-starting)): Let $0<\delta<1/100$, and put $$K_\delta=\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}, \qquad b=\frac18.$$ There are a smooth even function $B:\mathbb T\to[0,K_\delta]$ and, for every positive integer $N$, real numbers $Y_{N,k}\in[-1,1]$ $(0\le k<N)$ and a smooth function $B_N:\mathbb T\to\mathbb C$ such that $$\begin{gathered} B_N(-t)=\overline{B_N(t)},\qquad \|B_N(t)\|=B(t), \qquad \left\lVert U_{Y_N}-B_N\right\rVert_\infty=O_\delta(N^{-1}), \label{eq:starting-approximation}\\ \int_\mathbb TB(t)^2\,dt\ge1-7\delta, \qquad \frac1N\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Y_{N,k}^2\ge1-8\delta \quad\text{for all sufficiently large }N. \label{eq:starting-mass} \end{gathered}$$ The function $B$ vanishes near $0$ and $1/2$. Its superlevel set $\{t\in[0,1/2]:B(t)\ge b\}$ is a nonempty finite union of pairwise disjoint closed intervals of positive length, contained in $(0,1/2)$. Let $J_j=[u_j,v_j]$, $1\le j\le M$, be the closures of its complementary intervals in $[0,1/2]$, and put $$\label{eq:starting-gaps} l_j=v_j-u_j>0,\qquad L=\sum_{j=1}^M l_j,\qquad 0<L\le C_0\delta, \qquad C_0=10.$$ Thus $B\le b$ on the gaps $J_j$ and $B\ge b$ off their union.* *An endpoint $z$ of a gap other than $0$ and $1/2$ is called a *transition endpoint*. At each such $z$, $B(z)=b$, and on a neighborhood $V_z\subset(0,1/2)$ there are a real constant $\alpha_z$ and a real quadratic polynomial $\psi_z$ such that, for every $N$, $$\label{eq:starting-endpoint-phase} B_N(t)=B(t)\mathrm e\bigl(\alpha_z+N\psi_z(t)\bigr) \quad(t\in V_z),\qquad x_z:=\psi_z'(z)\in(0,1).$$ All objects except $Y_N$ and $B_N$ are fixed independently of $N$. Constants denoted by $O_\delta$ may depend on all the data chosen for this fixed $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Relaxed real coefficients approximate disjoint quadratic waves. Their common modulus has almost full mean-square mass, short gaps, and fixed quadratic phase descriptions at transition endpoints.
- **claim-quadratic-poisson-sampling** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sampling-poisson)): Let $\chi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$, let $x_*\in\mathbb R$ and $\lambda\in\mathbb R\setminus\{0\}$, and let $J\subset\mathbb R$ be compact. For $\beta\in J$ and $\ell\in\mathbb Z$, put $$x_{\beta,\ell}=x_*+\frac{\ell-\beta}{\lambda}, \qquad \omega_{\beta,\ell}=(\beta-\ell)x_* -\frac{(\beta-\ell)^2}{2\lambda}.$$ As $N$ tends to infinity through positive integers, uniformly for $\beta\in J$, $$\begin{aligned} &\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}\chi(k/N) \mathrm e\!\left(\beta k+\frac{N\lambda}{2}(k/N-x_*)^2\right) \notag\\ &\qquad= \frac1{\sqrt{\|\lambda\|}} \sum_{\ell\in\mathbb Z}\chi(x_{\beta,\ell}) \mathrm e\!\left(\frac{\operatorname{sgn}\lambda}{8} +N\omega_{\beta,\ell}\right) +O(N^{-1}). \label{eq:sampling-poisson} \end{aligned}$$ The sum on the right has nonzero terms in one fixed finite set of indices for all $\beta\in J$. The error constant depends only on $\chi,x_*,\lambda,J$.
  - Explanation: Poisson summation turns sampled quadratic phases into a uniformly approximating finite collection of stationary waves. A summable exterior estimate precedes the addition of stationary-phase errors.
- **claim-small-cost-gap-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-correction)): Fix $0<\delta<1/100$, and put $$b=\frac18, \qquad K_\delta=\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}.$$ Suppose that $B_N\in C^\infty(\mathbb T;\mathbb C)$, $N\in\mathbb N$, satisfy $B_N(-t)=\overline{B_N(t)}$, and that their common modulus $B=\|B_N\|\in C^\infty(\mathbb T)$ has the following properties.* 1. *$0\le B\le K_\delta$, and $B$ vanishes in neighborhoods of $0$ and $1/2$. The set $$E=\{t\in[0,1/2]:B(t)\ge b\}$$ is a nonempty finite union of disjoint closed intervals of positive length. The closures of its complementary intervals are $J_j=[u_j,v_j]$, $1\le j\le M$, with lengths $l_j=v_j-u_j>0$, and $$0<L:=\sum_{j=1}^M l_j\le 10\delta.$$* 2. *At every endpoint $z\in(0,1/2)$ of a gap $J_j$, there are a neighborhood of $z$, a real number $\alpha_z$, and a real quadratic polynomial $\psi_z$, all independent of $N$, such that $$B_N(t)=B(t)\mathrm e\bigl(\alpha_z+N\psi_z(t)\bigr), \qquad x_z:=\psi_z'(z)\in(0,1).$$* *There is an absolute constant $D$ such that, for every sufficiently large integer $N$, there is a continuous function $R_N:\mathbb T\to\mathbb C$ satisfying $R_N(-t)=\overline{R_N(t)}$, supported on the gaps and their reflections, for which $$\begin{aligned} b-o(1)&\le \|B_N(t)+R_N(t)\|\le K_\delta &&(t\in\mathbb T),\label{eq:corrected-envelope}\\ \max_{0\le k<N}\sqrt N\,\|\widehat R_N(k)\| &\le D\sqrt\delta,\label{eq:correction-coefficients}\\ \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0,\ldots,N-1\}}\|\widehat R_N(k)\| &=O_\delta(N^{-1/4}).\label{eq:correction-tail} \end{aligned}$$ Every Fourier coefficient $\widehat R_N(k)$ is real. The threshold for $N$ and the constants implicit in $o(1)$ and $O_\delta$ may depend on all the fixed input data; $D$ does not.
  - Explanation: A conjugate-symmetric correction raises the small-modulus gaps while preserving the upper envelope. Its individual coefficient cost is small and its exterior Fourier coefficient mass tends to zero.
- **claim-defect-sensitive-sign-rounding** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rounding)): There is an absolute constant $C$ with the following property. Let $N\ge1$ be an integer and let $X=(X_0,\ldots,X_{N-1})\in[-1,1]^N$. Put $$\mu(X)=\frac12\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(1-\|X_k\|).$$ Then there are signs $\varepsilon_0,\ldots,\varepsilon_{N-1}$ satisfying $$\label{eq:defect-rounding} \max_{t\in\mathbb T}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(\varepsilon_k-X_k)\mathrm e(kt)\right\| \le C\left(1+\sqrt{\mu(X)\log\frac{80N}{\mu(X)}}\right).$$ The square-root term is defined to be zero when $\mu(X)=0$. In that case one may take $\varepsilon=X$, giving zero error.
  - Explanation: Relaxed coefficients can be rounded to real signs with a uniform Fourier error controlled by their total defect mass. Already signed coefficients need no change.
- **claim-real-matrix-discrepancy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rounding-matrix)): There is an absolute constant $C_0$ such that, for integers $1\le s\le R$ and a real matrix $B\in[-1,1]^{R\times s}$, there is a vector $\xi\in\{-1,1\}^s$ satisfying $$\left\lVert B\xi\right\rVert_\infty\le C_0\sqrt{s\log(2R/s)}.$$
  - Explanation: Every bounded real matrix in the stated rectangular regime admits a sign vector with controlled maximum row sum. The proof uses an existential partial-coloring consequence and a compactness limit.

        "exact_quote": "For every $\\eta>0$ there is an integer $N_0=N_0(\\eta)$ such that, for every integer $N\\ge N_0$, there exist real signs $\\varepsilon_0,\\ldots,\\varepsilon_{N-1}$ for which $$\\frac1{16}\\sqrt N\n \\le\\left|\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\\varepsilon_kz^k\\right|\n \\le(1+\\eta)\\sqrt N\n \\qquad (|z|=1).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-076.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-auxiliary",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\delta<1/20$, there exist integers $m\\ge1$ and $r\\ge2$, nonzero vectors $a_1,\\ldots,a_r\\in\\mathbb Z^m$, a real-valued trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\\mathbb T^m$, and a vector $v\\in\\mathbb R^m$ with the following properties. No two of the vectors $a_i$ are parallel, and $$\\mathcal A=\\{a_1,-a_1,\\ldots,a_r,-a_r\\}$$ is a containing Fourier support of $F$. Moreover, $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-norms}\n \\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le1,\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert F\\right\\rVert_2\\ge1-3\\delta.$$ Writing $\\lambda_a=a\\cdot v$ for $a\\in\\mathcal A$, we have $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-widths}\n \\lambda_a\\ne0,\n \\qquad \\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal A}|\\lambda_a|<1,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{|\\widehat F(a)|}{\\sqrt{|\\lambda_a|}}\n \\le K_\\delta,\n \\qquad\n K_\\delta=\\sqrt{\\frac{(1+\\delta)^3}{1-\\delta}}.$$ All these objects depend on $\\delta$ alone.",
        "source_id": "PM-076.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R)$ and let $\\beta\\in\\mathbb R\\setminus\\{0\\}$ be fixed. As $T\\to\\infty$ through positive real values, $$\\label{eq:uniform-quadratic}\n \\begin{split}\n \\int_{\\mathbb R}g(x)\\mathrm e(T\\beta x^2/2-ux)\\,dx\n ={}&\\frac{\\mathrm e(\\operatorname{sgn}(\\beta)/8-u^2/(2T\\beta))}\n {\\sqrt{T|\\beta|}}\\\\\n &\\qquad\\cdot\\left(g\\left(\\frac{u}{T\\beta}\\right)\n       +O_{g,\\beta}(T^{-1})\\right),\n \\end{split}$$ uniformly for every real $u$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-signed-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $m\\ge1$ and $r\\ge2$ be integers, and let $a_1,\\ldots,a_r\\in\\mathbb Z^m\\setminus\\{0\\}$ be pairwise nonparallel.         "source_id": "PM-076.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring",
        "exact_quote": "For each integer $r\\ge2$ and each $\\gamma>0$, there is $\\beta>0$ such that the following holds. If an $r$-uniform hypergraph satisfies, for some $D>0$, $$(1-\\beta)D<d(v)<(1+\\beta)D\\quad\\text{for every vertex }v,\n \\qquad d(u,v)<\\beta D\\quad\\text{for }u\\ne v,$$ then its edges can be partitioned into at most $(1+\\gamma)D$ matchings.",
        "source_id": "PM-076.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-starting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0<\\delta<1/100$, and put $$K_\\delta=\\sqrt{\\frac{(1+\\delta)^3}{1-\\delta}},\n \\qquad b=\\frac18.$$ There are a smooth even function $B:\\mathbb T\\to[0,K_\\delta]$ and, for every positive integer $N$, real numbers $Y_{N,k}\\in[-1,1]$ $(0\\le k<N)$ and a smooth function $B_N:\\mathbb T\\to\\mathbb C$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n B_N(-t)=\\overline{B_N(t)},\\qquad |B_N(t)|=B(t),\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert U_{Y_N}-B_N\\right\\rVert_\\infty=O_\\delta(N^{-1}),\n \\label{eq:starting-approximation}\\\\\n \\int_\\mathbb TB(t)^2\\,dt\\ge1-7\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\frac1N\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Y_{N,k}^2\\ge1-8\\delta\n \\quad\\text{for all sufficiently large }N.\n \\label{eq:starting-mass}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The function $B$ vanishes near $0$ and $1/2$. Its superlevel set $\\{t\\in[0,1/2]:B(t)\\ge b\\}$ is a nonempty finite union of pairwise disjoint closed intervals of positive length, contained in $(0,1/2)$. Let $J_j=[u_j,v_j]$, $1\\le j\\le M$, be the closures of its complementary intervals in $[0,1/2]$, and put $$\\label{eq:starting-gaps}\n l_j=v_j-u_j>0,\\qquad\n L=\\sum_{j=1}^M l_j,\\qquad 0<L\\le C_0\\delta,\n \\qquad C_0=10.$$ Thus $B\\le b$ on the gaps $J_j$ and $B\\ge b$ off their union.*\n\n\n*An endpoint $z$ of a gap other than $0$ and $1/2$ is called a *transition endpoint*. At each such $z$, $B(z)=b$, and on a neighborhood $V_z\\subset(0,1/2)$ there are a real constant $\\alpha_z$ and a real quadratic polynomial $\\psi_z$ such that, for every $N$, $$\\label{eq:starting-endpoint-phase}\n B_N(t)=B(t)\\mathrm e\\bigl(\\alpha_z+N\\psi_z(t)\\bigr)\n \\quad(t\\in V_z),\\qquad\n x_z:=\\psi_z'(z)\\in(0,1).$$ All objects except $Y_N$ and $B_N$ are fixed independently of $N$. Constants denoted by $O_\\delta$ may depend on all the data chosen for this fixed $\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-076.2"
      },

The set $$E=\\{t\\in[0,1/2]:B(t)\\ge b\\}$$ is a nonempty finite union of disjoint closed intervals of positive length. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Littlewood polynomial** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *Littlewood polynomial of length $N$* is a polynomial $$P(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\varepsilon_kz^k, \qquad \varepsilon_k\in\{-1,1\}.$$
- **Torus and exponential notation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\mathbb T=\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ and $\mathrm e(t)=\exp(2\pi i t)$, and give $\mathbb T$ probability Haar measure.
- **Normalized polynomial and defect mass** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a real vector $X=(X_0,\ldots,X_{N-1})$, write $$U_X(t)=\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}X_k\mathrm e(kt), \qquad \mu(X)=\frac12\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(1-\|X_k\|) \quad\text{if }X\in[-1,1]^N.$$
- **Containing Fourier support** ([source](#sec:spreading)): A finite set is a *containing Fourier support* if every coefficient outside it vanishes; coefficients inside it are allowed to vanish as well.
- **Torus Fourier coefficient** ([source](#sec:spreading)): For a trigonometric polynomial $F$ on $\mathbb T^m$, write $$\widehat F(a)=\int_{\mathbb T^m}F(x)\mathrm e(-a\cdot x)\,dx, \qquad a\in\mathbb Z^m.$$
- **Closed torus interval** ([source](#sec:packing)): For $c\in\mathbb T$ and $0<\ell<1$, let $$I(c,\ell)=\{c+x\pmod 1:-\ell/2\le x\le\ell/2\}.$$
- **Uniform hypergraph** ([source](#sec:packing)): Recall that an $r$-uniform hypergraph consists of a finite vertex set and a family of distinct $r$-element subsets, called edges.
- **Hypergraph degree and codegree** ([source](#sec:packing)): Its degree $d(v)$ counts edges containing $v$, and its codegree $d(u,v)$ counts edges containing both distinct vertices $u,v$.
- **Matching** ([source](#sec:packing)): A matching is a family of pairwise disjoint edges.
- **Transition endpoint** ([source](#stmt-prop-starting)): (A structured starting construction). *Let $0<\delta<1/100$, and put $$K_\delta=\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}, \qquad b=\frac18.$$ There are a smooth even function $B:\mathbb T\to[0,K_\delta]$ and, for every positive integer $N$, real numbers $Y_{N,k}\in[-1,1]$ $(0\le k<N)$ and a smooth function $B_N:\mathbb T\to\mathbb C$ such that $$\begin{gathered} B_N(-t)=\overline{B_N(t)},\qquad \|B_N(t)\|=B(t), \qquad \left\lVert U_{Y_N}-B_N\right\rVert_\infty=O_\delta(N^{-1}), \label{eq:starting-approximation}\\ \int_\mathbb TB(t)^2\,dt\ge1-7\delta, \qquad \frac1N\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Y_{N,k}^2\ge1-8\delta \quad\text{for all sufficiently large }N. \label{eq:starting-mass} \end{gathered}$$ The function $B$ vanishes near $0$ and $1/2$. Its superlevel set $\{t\in[0,1/2]:B(t)\ge b\}$ is a nonempty finite union of pairwise disjoint closed intervals of positive length, contained in $(0,1/2)$. Let $J_j=[u_j,v_j]$, $1\le j\le M$, be the closures of its complementary intervals in $[0,1/2]$, and put $$\label{eq:starting-gaps} l_j=v_j-u_j>0,\qquad L=\sum_{j=1}^M l_j,\qquad 0<L\le C_0\delta, \qquad C_0=10.$$ Thus $B\le b$ on the gaps $J_j$ and $B\ge b$ off their union.* *An endpoint $z$ of a gap other than $0$ and $1/2$ is called a *transition endpoint*.
- **Derivative slot** ([source](#pm-h-22)): We call $D_j$ the derivative slot of $J_j$.

          "exact_quote": "An endpoint $z$ of a gap other than $0$ and $1/2$ is called a *transition endpoint*.",
          "source_id": "PM-076.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "(A structured starting construction). *Let $0<\\delta<1/100$, and put $$K_\\delta=\\sqrt{\\frac{(1+\\delta)^3}{1-\\delta}}, \\qquad b=\\frac18.$$ There are a smooth even function $B:\\mathbb T\\to[0,K_\\delta]$ and, for every positive integer $N$, real numbers $Y_{N,k}\\in[-1,1]$ $(0\\le k<N)$ and a smooth function $B_N:\\mathbb T\\to\\mathbb C$ such that $$\\begin{gathered} B_N(-t)=\\overline{B_N(t)},\\qquad |B_N(t)|=B(t), \\qquad \\left\\lVert U_{Y_N}-B_N\\right\\rVert_\\infty=O_\\delta(N^{-1}), \\label{eq:starting-approximation}\\\\ \\int_\\mathbb TB(t)^2\\,dt\\ge1-7\\delta, \\qquad \\frac1N\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}Y_{N,k}^2\\ge1-8\\delta \\quad\\text{for all sufficiently large }N. \\label{eq:starting-mass} \\end{gathered}$$ The function $B$ vanishes near $0$ and $1/2$. Its superlevel set $\\{t\\in[0,1/2]:B(t)\\ge b\\}$ is a nonempty finite union of pairwise disjoint closed intervals of positive length, contained in $(0,1/2)$. Let $J_j=[u_j,v_j]$, $1\\le j\\le M$, be the closures of its complementary intervals in $[0,1/2]$, and put $$\\label{eq:starting-gaps} l_j=v_j-u_j>0,\\qquad L=\\sum_{j=1}^M l_j,\\qquad 0<L\\le C_0\\delta, \\qquad C_0=10.$$ Thus $B\\le b$ on the gaps $J_j$ and $B\\ge b$ off their union.* *An endpoint $z$ of a gap other than $0$ and $1/2$ is called a *transition endpoint*.",
      "interpretation": "Interior gap endpoints carry the local quadratic phase data needed to turn on the correction without cancellation.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Transition endpoint",

      "interpretation": "Each gap receives a separate interval of leading phase derivatives, limiting stationary contributions to a Fourier coefficient.",
      "kind": "definition",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-lower-modulus | Uniform modulus of the selected sign polynomial | ≥ | $\frac1{16}\sqrt N$ | The threshold depends on the prescribed upper accuracy; the lower constant is fixed. |
| q-main-upper-modulus | Uniform modulus of the selected sign polynomial | ≤ | $(1+\eta)\sqrt N$ | The upper accuracy is fixed before the length grows. |
| q-auxiliary-mass | \left\lVert F\right\rVert_2 | ≥ | $1-3\delta$ | All auxiliary objects depend on the fixed accuracy parameter alone. |
| q-signed-width-budget | \sum_{a\in\mathcal A}\|\lambda_a\| | < | $1$ | Both signs are already included in the sum. |
| q-coefficient-width-ratio | \frac{\|\widehat F(a)\|}{\sqrt{\|\lambda_a\|}} | ≤ | $K_\delta$ | The controlling constant depends on the accuracy parameter. |
| q-envelope-constant | K_\delta | = | $\sqrt{\frac{(1+\delta)^3}{1-\delta}}$ | The accuracy parameter remains fixed during the length limit. |
| q-starting-approximation | \left\lVert U_{Y_N}-B_N\right\rVert_\infty | = | $O_\delta(N^{-1})$ | The implicit constant may depend on all data fixed with the accuracy parameter. |
| q-gap-length | L | ≤ | $10\delta$ | The gaps and their geometry are fixed independently of the sampling length. |
| q-correction-coefficient-cost | \max_{0\le k<N}\sqrt N\,\|\widehat R_N(k)\| | ≤ | $D\sqrt\delta$ | The controlling constant is absolute; the threshold may depend on fixed input data. |
| q-correction-exterior-tail | \sum_{k\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0,\ldots,N-1\}}\|\widehat R_N(k)\| | = | $O_\delta(N^{-1/4})$ | The implicit constant may depend on all fixed input data. |
| q-transition-strip-width | w | = | $N^{-3/4}$ | The length grows after the gap geometry and phase data have been fixed. |
| q-defect-rounding-error | Uniform unnormalized Fourier rounding error | ≤ | $C\left(1+\sqrt{\mu(X)\log\frac{80N}{\mu(X)}}\right)$ | The constant is absolute; the square-root term is defined as zero at zero defect. |
| q-matrix-sign-discrepancy | \left\lVert B\xi\right\rVert_\infty | ≤ | $C_0\sqrt{s\log(2R/s)}$ | The discrepancy constant is absolute. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-quadratic-integral** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadratic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic): Complete the square, regularize the Gaussian integral, and use dominated convergence on the Fourier side to obtain an error independent of the translated stationary point. -> Uniform quadratic integration estimate.
- **step-spread-and-truncate** [claim](#stmt-prop-auxiliary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-2): Build an almost full-mass bounded polynomial recursively, tune quadratic curvatures, spread coefficients in additional variables, and truncate with a summable exterior bound. -> An auxiliary polynomial with nonparallel signed support and controlled widths.
- **step-external-edge-coloring** [claim](#stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring) / [proof](#sec:packing): Invoke the stated external edge-coloring theorem and select a largest matching from the resulting partition. -> A matching covering almost all vertices in the asymptotic regime.
- **step-pack-signed-arcs** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-packing-edge-coloring): Place randomly shifted and oriented slots on prime grids, estimate conditional moments, delete exceptional vertices, select a large matching, and shrink intervals strictly inside slots. -> Fixed torus centers with pairwise disjoint signed closed intervals.
- **step-poisson-sampling** [claim](#stmt-lem-sampling-poisson) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampling-poisson): Apply Poisson summation, bound the nonstationary lattice tail by integration by parts, and apply uniform quadratic integration only to the fixed finite remaining set. -> Uniform quadratic sampling formula.
- **step-starting-waves** [claim](#stmt-prop-starting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampling-poisson-2): Sample the auxiliary polynomial along quadratic paths with separate cutoffs. Disjoint supporting arcs make the modulus independent of length and allow an exact mass calculation and gap description. -> Relaxed coefficients, fixed envelope, short gaps, and local endpoint phases.
- **step-gap-correction** [claim](#stmt-prop-correction) / [proof](#sec:correction): Assign disjoint derivative slots, join to endpoint derivatives with steep outer pieces, match full endpoint phases, taper the amplitudes, and estimate stationary coefficients and exterior tails separately. -> A corrected positive envelope with small coefficient cost and summable exterior error.
- **step-matrix-discrepancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-rounding-matrix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rounding-matrix): Use the existential partial-coloring consequence, pass to a compactness limit to fix coordinates exactly, and repeat while summing geometrically decreasing row errors. -> A sign vector with the stated row discrepancy bound.
- **step-defect-rounding** [claim](#stmt-lem-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rounding-matrix-2): Round defects on successively coarser dyadic grids, reverse discrepancy signs to prevent mass increase, and transfer the Fourier grid estimate to the whole circle by a derivative bound. -> Real signs with defect-sensitive uniform Fourier error.
- **step-complete-flatness** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rounding-matrix-3): Project the correction onto the permitted degrees, rescale into the coefficient cube, estimate defect mass, round, and choose accuracy before increasing the length. -> A single real sign polynomial satisfying both uniform modulus bounds.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-simultaneous-circle-bounds**: The same real sign polynomial has a fixed positive lower modulus and an upper modulus arbitrarily close to the Parseval lower bound, uniformly on the entire unit circle at every sufficiently large length.
- **invariant-strict-spectral-width**: The auxiliary polynomial has bounded supremum norm and almost full mean-square mass while its signed containing Fourier support satisfies a strict total width budget and controlled coefficient-to-width ratios.
- **invariant-correction-preserves-coefficient-budget**: The gap correction raises the lower envelope while preserving conjugate symmetry, bounds each permitted Fourier coefficient, and has a summable exterior Fourier tail tending to zero.
- **invariant-defect-controls-rounding**: Rounding relaxed real coefficients to signs incurs a uniform Fourier error controlled by their total defect mass. If the defect vanishes, the existing coefficients already give signs with zero error.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-short-length-generator**: Treat the main theorem as a certified generator for a prescribed short sign sequence. -> The theorem guarantees existence only beyond an accuracy-dependent threshold and supplies no explicit threshold or finite generation contract.
- **anti-use-independent-sign-choices**: Combine a polynomial with a nearly minimal maximum and a separately chosen polynomial with a positive minimum. -> Bounds proved for different choices of signs do not establish simultaneous bounds for a single polynomial.
- **anti-use-unmatched-gap-wave**: Repair every small-modulus interval by adding an oscillatory wave with arbitrary endpoint phase. -> The added wave can cancel the existing wave while its amplitude turns on, destroying the lower envelope.
- **anti-use-coefficient-bound-without-tail-control**: Truncate the correction to the permitted degrees using only its individual coefficient bound. -> An individual coefficient estimate does not bound the sum of discarded Fourier coefficients or the resulting uniform truncation error.
- **anti-use-grid-as-circle-certificate**: Use agreement at finitely many angle samples as a certificate of the full uniform rounding bound. -> Sampled values alone do not control values between grid points.
- **anti-use-optimal-lower-flatness**: Read the main theorem as proving that the normalized modulus tends uniformly to its optimal value. -> The lower constant is fixed and does not approach the upper constant as the requested upper accuracy improves.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an asymptotic existence theorem with an unspecified accuracy-dependent length threshold and a uniform conclusion on the entire unit circle. The auxiliary construction uses limiting estimates, and the discrepancy argument obtains exact signs by a compactness limit. Its proof explicitly states: “The cited theorem finds such a point with positive probability; we use only its existence assertion.” Finite enumeration of selected signs or sampled angles would not certify this result, and the paper does not supply a finite executable construction with an explicit threshold and discrepancy constant.

| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The matching argument selects compatible slots without reuse. It supplies no distributed protocol, communication model, or guarantee under asynchronous execution. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-asymptotic-length-threshold** (hypothesis): The main conclusion holds beyond an accuracy-dependent length threshold; it does not certify an arbitrary prescribed short length. Blocked use: ['Generate a short sign sequence with the claimed bounds without first establishing that its length is admissible.']
- **limit-real-sign-circle-scope** (formal_scope): The simultaneous bounds concern a polynomial with consecutive real sign coefficients evaluated on the unit circle. Blocked use: ['Transfer the modulus bounds to arbitrary complex coefficients or to evaluations away from the unit circle.']
- **limit-coordinated-sign-choice** (hypothesis): The upper and lower modulus bounds must be achieved by the same polynomial. Blocked use: ['Combine separate existence results whose favorable bounds hold for different choices of coefficients.']
- **limit-fixed-positive-lower-bound** (formal_scope): The lower constant remains fixed as the upper accuracy improves; the theorem does not establish an asymptotically optimal lower modulus. Blocked use: ['Claim that the normalized modulus tends uniformly to its Parseval scale.']
- **limit-auxiliary-width-and-support** (hypothesis): Coefficient spreading requires the stated accuracy regime, nonzero widths, strict total width slack, and pairwise nonparallel signed frequency representatives. A containing support may include vanishing coefficients. Blocked use: ['Use a frequency family with zero widths or exhausted width slack as an input to the subsequent packing.', 'Treat every frequency in the containing support as having a nonzero coefficient.']
- **limit-quadratic-integration-regime** (hypothesis): The uniform quadratic estimate fixes a smooth compactly supported amplitude and a nonzero curvature before the oscillation parameter tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Apply the same error constant to nonsmooth amplitudes, zero curvature, or varying curvature without additional estimates.']
- **limit-signed-packing-preconditions** (hypothesis): Signed interval packing requires positive widths with strict total slack and nonzero pairwise nonparallel integer forms. Blocked use: ['Assert disjoint closed intervals for parallel forms or for widths lacking the prescribed strict slack.']
- **limit-hypergraph-matching-input** (hypothesis): The edge partition theorem assumes a uniform hypergraph with degrees close to a common scale and codegrees below its stated threshold. Blocked use: ['Infer a large compatible matching solely from the number of vertices while ignoring degree and codegree conditions.']
- **limit-sampling-uniformity** (hypothesis): The sampling formula requires a smooth compactly supported cutoff, fixed nonzero curvature, and a compact modulation range. Its finite leading sum and error constant are tied to those fixed data. Blocked use: ['Extend the uniform sampling error to an unbounded modulation range or changing cutoff data without re-establishing the tail estimates.']
- **limit-starting-envelope-has-gaps** (edge_case): The starting envelope vanishes near the real points of the circle and therefore does not itself furnish the desired positive lower bound. Blocked use: ['Round the starting coefficients and claim the main lower modulus without constructing the gap correction.']
- **limit-correction-geometry-and-phase** (hypothesis): The correction assumes a common smooth conjugate-symmetric envelope, finite short-gap geometry, and fixed local quadratic phases with interior endpoint derivatives. Short gap length alone does not supply the endpoint alignment needed to prevent cancellation. Blocked use: ['Repair an arbitrary small-modulus set by adding waves with unrelated endpoint phases or derivatives.']
- **limit-taper-scale-and-fixed-data** (approximation): The taper width must preserve phase alignment while allowing the exterior Fourier tail to vanish. Auxiliary geometry is fixed before the length limit; the estimates do not assert uniformity over changing gap data. Blocked use: ['Choose taper widths without checking both limiting requirements.', 'Let the gap geometry vary with length while retaining the same implicit error bounds.']
- **limit-projection-requires-absolute-tail** (approximation): Restricting the correction to the permitted degrees preserves its uniform effect through the absolute exterior Fourier-tail estimate. The individual coefficient bound alone does not control the discarded sum. Blocked use: ['Project the correction using only an individual coefficient estimate or a mean-square tail estimate.']
- **limit-rounding-cube-and-zero-defect** (edge_case): Defect-sensitive rounding assumes a bounded real coefficient vector. At zero defect the square-root term has a separate convention and the original coefficients already give signs. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the displayed logarithmic expression directly at zero defect.', 'Apply the defect formula as a rounding guarantee for coefficients outside the real coefficient cube.']
- **limit-matrix-discrepancy-and-compactness** (nonconstructive): The matrix lemma assumes bounded real entries and no more columns than rows. Its proof uses an existential partial coloring and a compactness limit, rather than supplying a finite implementation of the exact selection step. Blocked use: ['Claim an executable deterministic discrepancy procedure or a running-time bound from this proof.', 'Apply the displayed discrepancy formula outside its matrix regime without a separate argument.']
- **limit-grid-transfer-and-mass-preservation** (hypothesis): Dyadic rounding controls the moving coordinates by preventing the defect mass from increasing. The finite Fourier grid is then transferred to the entire circle using a derivative estimate for the rounding error polynomial. Blocked use: ['Replace the mass-preserving sign reversal by unrestricted coordinate rounding while retaining the defect-sensitive bound.', 'Treat finite angle samples alone as a certificate of the uniform circle estimate.']
- **limit-completion-order-and-rescaling** (hypothesis): Completion projects the correction, rescales the coefficients into the real coefficient cube, controls their defect, and only then rounds. The accuracy parameter and associated auxiliary data are chosen before the length becomes sufficiently large. Blocked use: ['Round unrescaled corrected coefficients without checking coefficient bounds.', 'Exchange the accuracy and length limits without quantitative estimates uniform in the changing auxiliary data.']

          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-rounding-matrix</summary>\n\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.* Let $b=1/8$ and let $D>0$ be the absolute constant in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:correction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:correction\">8</a>. For a fixed sufficiently small $\\delta>0$, take the real coefficients $Y_{N,k}$ and the functions $B_N$ from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:starting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:starting\">6</a>, and the correction $R_N$ from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:correction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:correction\">8</a>. All estimates below hold for every sufficiently large integer $N$. Put $$s_\\delta=D\\sqrt\\delta,\\qquad S_\\delta=1+s_\\delta,\n \\qquad\n X_{N,k}=\\frac{Y_{N,k}+\\sqrt N\\,\\widehat R_N(k)}{S_\\delta}\n \\quad(0\\le k<N).$$ Conjugate symmetry of $R_N$ makes $\\widehat R_N(k)$ real. Since $|Y_{N,k}|\\le1$ and $\\sqrt N|\\widehat R_N(k)|\\le s_\\delta$, the vector $X_N$ belongs to $[-1,1]^N$. The uniform approximation in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:starting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:starting\">6</a> and the exterior Fourier-tail estimate in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:correction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:correction\">8</a> give $$\\label{eq:completion-profile}\n U_{X_N}(t)=\\frac{B_N(t)+R_N(t)}{S_\\delta}+o(1)\n \\qquad\\text{uniformly for }t\\in\\mathbb T.$$ Consequently, $$\\label{eq:completion-envelope}\n \\min_{t\\in\\mathbb T}|U_{X_N}(t)|\\ge\\frac b{S_\\delta}-o(1),\n \\qquad\n \\left\\lVert U_{X_N}\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le K_\\delta+o(1).$$ In the second inequality we used $S_\\delta\\ge1$.\n\n\nThe same coefficient bound controls the cost of rounding. Directly, $$|X_{N,k}-Y_{N,k}|\n =\\frac{|\\sqrt N\\,\\widehat R_N(k)-s_\\delta Y_{N,k}|}{S_\\delta}\n \\le2s_\\delta.$$ Both coefficients lie in $[-1,1]$, so $|X_{N,k}^2-Y_{N,k}^2|\\le4s_\\delta$. Using the mean-square estimate from Proposition <a href=\"#prop:starting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:starting\">6</a> and $1-|x|\\le1-x^2$ for $|x|\\le1$, we obtain $$\\label{eq:completion-defect}\n \\frac{\\mu(X_N)}N\n \\le\\frac1{2N}\\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(1-X_{N,k}^2)\n \\le4\\delta+2D\\sqrt\\delta=:q_\\delta.$$ Choose $\\delta$ small enough that $q_\\delta<1/2$. The function $q\\mapsto q\\log(80/q)$ is increasing on $(0,1/2]$. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:rounding\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:rounding\">9</a> therefore supplies signs $\\varepsilon_0,\\ldots,\\varepsilon_{N-1}$ with $$\\label{eq:completion-rounding}\n \\left\\lVert U_\\varepsilon-U_{X_N}\\right\\rVert_\\infty\n \\le \\frac C{\\sqrt N}\n       +C\\sqrt{q_\\delta\\log(80/q_\\delta)}.$$ If $\\mu(X_N)=0$, we instead take $\\varepsilon_k=X_{N,k}$, so this bound also covers that case.\n\nFix $c=1/16$ once and for all. As $\\delta\\downarrow0$, $$\\frac b{S_\\delta}\n       -C\\sqrt{q_\\delta\\log(80/q_\\delta)}\\longrightarrow\\frac18,\n \\qquad\n K_\\delta+C\\sqrt{q_\\delta\\log(80/q_\\delta)}\\longrightarrow1.$$ For any prescribed $\\eta>0$, first choose $\\delta$ so small that the first expression is strictly larger than $c$ and the second is strictly smaller than $1+\\eta$. Fix every construction datum associated with this $\\delta$. We may then choose $N_0$ so that for every $N\\ge N_0$ all preceding estimates hold and the errors in <a href=\"#eq:completion-envelope\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:completion-envelope\">[eq:completion-envelope]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:completion-rounding\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:completion-rounding\">[eq:completion-rounding]</a> are smaller than these strict margins. It follows that $$c\\le|U_\\varepsilon(t)|\\le1+\\eta\\qquad(t\\in\\mathbb T).$$ Multiplication by $\\sqrt N$ gives the asserted polynomial. Rounding assigns a real sign to each of the consecutive indices $0,\\ldots,N-1$; all estimates include $t=0$ and $t=1/2$ and hold through every sufficiently large integer length. ◻",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every <i>η</i> &gt; 0 and every sufficiently large integer <i>N</i>, there is a polynomial with <i>N</i> consecutive coefficients in $`\{-1,1\}`$ whose modulus lies between $`\sqrt N/16`$ and $`(1+\eta)\sqrt N`$ everywhere on the unit circle.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
